# Gazetteer — every place named so far

*Private working file. Canonical names are given exactly as they must be written with `\place{...}` in the scenes. Distances are in leagues (a league is three Sarnish miles, a little under three kilometres), and direction is measured from **Sill** unless the entry says otherwise. Where a place lies inside another place, the containing place is given first in the relations.*

---

## I. The world and the great powers

### Sarn
**Kind:** the world. **Region:** all.
The bowl of warm land between the Hearth beneath and the Waste beyond the Rim; every vessel in it is a small failing dam.
**Relations:** The Hearth lies beneath the Keel to the south-east; the Waste begins beyond the Rim to the west; the Sallow Sea fills the north-east.

### The Hearth
**Kind:** source, chamber of stone. **Region:** beneath the Hearthseat, on the Keel.
The great source of the Warm, said to hold what the world had when it was made; its fire is kept behind iron in the Weighing Hall.
**Relations:** Beneath the Hearthseat, about 150 leagues south-east of Sill by the Clay and Hearth Roads; the Nine Wells lie within twenty leagues of it.

### The Waste
**Kind:** waste, cold country outside the world. **Region:** beyond the Rim.
The great sink where everything unheld goes; travellers who go far into it come back older and thinner.
**Relations:** Begins beyond the Rim, roughly 200 leagues west of Sill; no Court collectors go there.

### The Rim
**Kind:** mountain chain. **Region:** west and north of the bowl.
The cold mountains that ring Sarn, broken by a few passes; the Listening Stones stand in its high places.
**Relations:** Nearest spur about 120 leagues west-north-west of Sill; the Cold Pass cuts it at 130 leagues; Keld Keep lies against it.

### The Keel
**Kind:** low ridge. **Region:** centre-south-east of the bowl.
A long low ridge of stone over the Hearth, mildly warm in winter and therefore crowded.
**Relations:** About 140 leagues south-east of Sill; the Hearthseat stands on it; the Nine Wells rise in it.

### The Sallow Sea
**Kind:** inland sea. **Region:** north-east.
A cold inland sea the colour of skimmed milk, whose tides run against the wind and are said to follow the Warm rather than the moon.
**Relations:** Its shore lies about 70 leagues north-east of Sill; the Ilm drains into it; Vhal stands on its coast at 80 leagues; the Glaze Cliffs are on the same shore.

### The Warm Court
**Kind:** realm, institution, church. **Region:** everywhere in theory; the Hearthseat in fact.
The Keeping: assessors, collectors and bankers who own the Hearth, the Returns and the slates, and speak of theft as accounts.
**Relations:** Seated in the Hearthseat, 150 leagues south-east of Sill; its factor in Greybank is 18 leagues east of Sill and is the Court's nearest physical presence to Grath.

### The Hearthseat
**Kind:** city. **Region:** the Keel.
The Court's city, built over the Hearth, where the Weighing Hall, the sealed files and fewer than twenty Kept vessels are kept.
**Relations:** 150 leagues south-east of Sill: 18 leagues east by the Clay Road to Greybank, then 140 leagues south-east by the Hearth Road; the Keel under it; the Nine Wells within twenty leagues.

### The Weighing Hall
**Kind:** hall, temple and counting room. **Region:** the Hearthseat.
The room where the year's measures are read at the Weighing and where the Hearth-fire burns behind a grating of cold iron.
**Relations:** Inside the Hearthseat, above the Hearth chamber.

### The College of Assessors
**Kind:** academy, counting house. **Region:** the Hearthseat, on the Keel.
The school where the value of a life is taught and Ledgerhand written; it holds the only right to enter a lawful deduction and keeps a second file of names removed from the rolls. Provost Wessen teaches here.
**Relations:** In Slate Row in the Hearthseat, 150 leagues south-east of Sill; an assessor's house stands in Sill's own Slate Row, built in Y.H. 776.

*The close ground of the Court city drawn on `atlas/the-hearthseat`: the rooms a surveyor of the Keeping could put a name to. Direction is from the Weighing Hall.*

### Slate Row of the Hearthseat
**Kind:** court quarter, street. **Region:** the Hearthseat, on the Keel.
The Hearthseat's own Court quarter, holding the College of Assessors and the officers' counting rooms; the year's measures are read aloud here at the Weighing. On a chart it is lettered simply *Slate Row*, but in scenes it must be told apart from Sill's quarter of the same name: `\place[Slate Row of the Hearthseat]{Slate Row}`.
**Relations:** Inside the Hearthseat, 150 leagues south-east of Sill; the College of Assessors stands here.

### The Hearth-fire
**Kind:** flame, wonder. **Region:** the Weighing Hall, the Hearthseat.
The never-out flame behind a grating of cold iron, said to be a tongue of the Hearth drawn up through the Keel; it has never gone out and no priest has ever been allowed to touch it. Even the Kept are not asked to stand near it.
**Relations:** In the Weighing Hall, above the Hearth chamber; 150 leagues south-east of Sill.

### The Weighing notice
**Kind:** printed notice, board. **Region:** the Hearthseat.
The notice set up by the Weighing Hall on which one part in twelve is printed as the deduction; it is the figure the districts read and the figure the College teaches its own to write against.
**Relations:** Beside the Weighing Hall in the Hearthseat.

### The Steward's room
**Kind:** room, office. **Region:** the Hearthseat.
The room of the head of the Keeping, off the Weighing Hall, where a slate is kept flat under the fingertips while a person is still speaking and where the sealed file is read.
**Relations:** Inside the Hearthseat, beside the Weighing Hall.

### The sealed files
**Kind:** archive, strong-room. **Region:** the Hearthseat.
The Court's locked archive, kept by the Steward of the Hearth. It holds the copy of the Keld survey's crossed-out entry of Y.H. 588, the private authorisation of a whole return of forty unspent measures with the purchaser left unrecorded, and the Court's private answer to the Unbanked. Haldre's suppressed paper is kept in a drawer beside it.
**Relations:** Inside the Hearthseat, near the Weighing Hall.

### The bank at the Hearthseat
**Kind:** bank, hall. **Region:** the Hearthseat.
The first of the Keeping's two banks, where measures drawn in the districts are pressed into newborn vessels; the second stands at Greybank. The rolls of the dead are carried forward here and never closed.
**Relations:** Inside the Hearthseat on the Keel; the bank at Greybank is 150 leagues west-north-west by road.

### The rooms of the Kept
**Kind:** chambers. **Region:** the Hearthseat.
Fewer than twenty rooms kept near the fire for the Kept vessels, who hold three hundred measures and feel nothing at all.
**Relations:** Inside the Hearthseat, on the Keel.

### The back rooms of the Hearthseat
**Kind:** work rooms. **Region:** the Hearthseat.
Plain rooms behind the College where blent-work is said to be done — condemned in law and spoken of only in the back rooms. The law gives a blent person the measure of the vessel its donor had, which in practice means no measure at all.
**Relations:** Inside the Hearthseat, behind the College of Assessors in Slate Row.

### Vhalen
**Kind:** realm, merchant republic. **Region:** Sallow coast.
A ring of port towns governed by a Council of Tides, living on salt, glass, rope, ships and contracts written on paper that rots.
**Relations:** North-east of Sill, 80 leagues by the Salt Road; capital Vhal on the Sallow shore; the Glaze Cliffs on the same coast.

### Vhal
**Kind:** port city. **Region:** Vhalen, Sallow coast.
The principal Vhaleni port, seat of the Council of Tides, with salt pans, glass furnaces and the tar-smelling council room.
**Relations:** 80 leagues north-east of Sill; at the northern end of the Salt Road; the Glaze Cliffs about five leagues along the coast.

### The Quay
**Kind:** district, waterfront. **Region:** Vhal, Vhalen.
The working waterfront of Vhal where boat-shares are held and pass through mothers; a man who marries into a boat-owning family is said to be come aboard and his opinion on the boat is counted at half. Pia of the Quay's mother holds a share here.
**Relations:** Inside Vhal, on the Sallow shore, 80 leagues north-east of Sill.

### The Salt Pans
**Kind:** works, salt flats. **Region:** Vhalen, Sallow coast.
Shallow pans where sea water is left to sun and wind; the dippers bless the nets here at dawn and read the Sallow draw. Contracts written on salt-paper rot in the damp, which is considered a virtue.
**Relations:** Outside Vhal on the Sallow shore; the Glaze Cliffs lie a few leagues along the same coast.

### The Council room
**Kind:** council chamber, hall. **Region:** Vhal, Vhalen.
The Council of Tides meets in a room that smells of tar and wet rope; its decrees are written on salt-paper and rot in damp weather, which is held to be a virtue.
**Relations:** Inside Vhal, on the Quay, beside the Sallow shore, 80 leagues north-east of Sill.

### The tide-tally house
**Kind:** counting house. **Region:** the Quay, Vhal.
The house on the Quay where the tide-tallies are kept and where Kett of the Quay reads the draws against the salt-paper contracts; a sheet left in the damp is a debt made nothing.
**Relations:** On the Quay in Vhal, a short way from the Council room.

### The glass furnaces
**Kind:** works, furnaces. **Region:** Vhal, Vhalen.
The furnaces of Vhal where sand and lead from the Glaze Cliffs are melted into glass for sale as far as Sere; the men who work them revolted in Y.H. 626.
**Relations:** Inside Vhal, on the shore side east of the Quay; the Glaze Cliffs lie about five leagues along the coast.

### The pulled benches
**Kind:** galley benches, quay. **Region:** the Quay, Vhal.
The benches of the salt galleys where condemned criminals row a term of seven years, or until their hands give out; a Vhaleni hull is never long from the oars.
**Relations:** At the Quay in Vhal, beside the moored salt boats.

### The coast pass
**Kind:** pass, road. **Region:** between Greybank and Vhal, Sallow coast.
The low pass on the Salt Road where the clay country gives way to the salt coast; the road from Sill reaches Vhal by Greybank and this pass in eighty leagues.
**Relations:** On the Salt Road between Greybank and Vhal, roughly a day's walk south-west of Vhal.

### Keld
**Kind:** realm, mountain kingdom. **Region:** the Rim.
A high kingdom of iron, lead, coal and warm-stone, ruled by the Warden of the Rim, whose surveyors keep the record of the rim-seam.
**Relations:** 120 leagues west-north-west of Sill by the Rim Road; capital Keld Keep; the Cold Pass beyond it at 130 leagues.

### Keld Keep
**Kind:** fortress city. **Region:** Keld, the Rim.
The Warden's seat, where a man must state his measure before his name and where the deep survey shaft begins.
**Relations:** 120 leagues west-north-west of Sill; the First Seam beneath it; the Listening Stones about five leagues higher into the Rim; the Cold Pass ten leagues beyond.

*The Keep above and the deep survey beneath it drawn on `atlas/keld`: the walled city, the Warden's hall and the shaft mouth; the Cold Pass and the Listening Stones; the First Seam with its galleries, Duelling Floor, survey room and Ninth Gallery. Direction is from the Warden's hall.*

### The Warden's hall
**Kind:** hall, seat. **Region:** Keld Keep, Keld.
The Warden's hall inside the walls of Keld Keep, marked by a standard, where the Warden of the Rim holds his court and a man states his measure before his name.
**Relations:** Inside Keld Keep, near the mine-reeve's room and the shaft mouth.

### The mine-reeve's room
**Kind:** counting room. **Region:** Keld Keep, Keld.
The room in Keld Keep where the mine-reeve checks a man who has lied about his measure and counts every ember of iron and every life that goes down.
**Relations:** Inside Keld Keep, beside the Warden's hall.

### The shaft mouth
**Kind:** entrance, timbered head of a mine. **Region:** Keld Keep, Keld.
The timbered opening within Keld Keep where the rope goes down into the First Seam and where a child's Descent begins.
**Relations:** Inside Keld Keep, below the Warden's hall; the First Seam descends from it.

### The First Seam
**Kind:** mine, survey shaft. **Region:** Keld, beneath Keld Keep.
The deep working where the Keldish survey the oldest repairs and where a crossed-out entry of Y.H. 588 records a man with a branching red mark. Siv of the First Seam keeps the survey here.
**Relations:** Beneath Keld Keep, 120 leagues west-north-west of Sill.

### The Duelling Floor
**Kind:** duelling floor, chamber. **Region:** the First Seam, Keld Keep.
A floor cut into the stone of the deep shaft where the Order of the Tenth Clamp fights with iron clamps and where a young Keldish person first opens a way in the forearm; Hald Iron is master of the floor. A surviving copy of Haldre's suppressed paper is kept nearby in a tin lined with warm-stone.
**Relations:** Inside the First Seam, beneath Keld Keep, 120 leagues west-north-west of Sill.

### The Listening Stones
**Kind:** standing stones, survey site. **Region:** high Rim, Keld.
Old stones against which the Vorr sit for days and come away with numbers; the Keldish surveyors use them to read the rim-seam.
**Relations:** About five leagues higher into the Rim from Keld Keep, some 125 leagues west-north-west of Sill.

### The Cold Pass
**Kind:** mountain pass, border. **Region:** the Rim, between Keld and Grath.
The pass closed by Karric VII in Y.H. 741 and fixed as the northern border by the agreement of Y.H. 747.
**Relations:** About 130 leagues west-north-west of Sill; ten leagues beyond Keld Keep on the Rim Road.

### The survey post
**Kind:** survey post, marker. **Region:** the Cold Pass, the Rim.
The survey post at the Cold Pass, set where the border was fixed in Y.H. 747; there is a post and no gate, and the Warden's surveyors do not promise to bring a man back.
**Relations:** At the Cold Pass, ten leagues beyond Keld Keep on the Rim Road.

### The Descent
**Kind:** passage, initiation way. **Region:** the First Seam, Keld Keep.
The way down the First Seam that every Keldish child takes at thirteen, when the measure is cut into the forearm and a warm-stone is given after the knife; a man who returns thin has the old numbers cut out and new ones put in.
**Relations:** From the shaft mouth in Keld Keep into the First Seam, beneath Keld Keep.

### The Ninth Gallery
**Kind:** mine gallery. **Region:** the First Seam, Keld Keep.
A deep gallery of the First Seam where the warm-stone is worked and where a leech of the gallery kept his remedies on the backs of survey sheets; the rock here sweats and the smell of hot lead gives the only warning.
**Relations:** Inside the First Seam, beneath Keld Keep, deeper than the Duelling Floor.

### The survey room
**Kind:** survey room, counting room. **Region:** the First Seam, Keld Keep.
The room off the Duelling Floor where the surveyors of the rim-seam keep their tally and where the crossed-out entry of Y.H. 588, with its branching red mark, is shown to those allowed to read it.
**Relations:** Off the Duelling Floor in the First Seam, beneath Keld Keep.

### The tin lined with warm-stone
**Kind:** tin, holding. **Region:** the survey room, the First Seam, Keld Keep.
The tin lined with warm-stone, kept in the survey room off the Duelling Floor, holding a surviving copy of Haldre's suppressed paper. The Order of the Tenth Clamp reads it once a year and does not act on it.
**Relations:** Inside the survey room, off the Duelling Floor in the First Seam, beneath Keld Keep.

### Sere
**Kind:** empire. **Region:** the far south.
A hot empire of grain, wine, bowls and open chattel slavery, whose people teach that a vessel was made to be emptied gloriously.
**Relations:** 300 leagues south of Sill; its northern reach stops at the Peat Line; the capital is the Bowl of Sere.

### The Bowl of Sere
**Kind:** imperial city. **Region:** Sere.
The capital, a city of open courts and public fires where debt-slates are burnt by lot and returned slaves are exhibited.
**Relations:** About 300 leagues south of Sill; the Peat Line is roughly 200 leagues north of it.

### The Ember bowl-house
**Kind:** great house. **Region:** the Bowl of Sere.
A house of the Ten Bowls that buys poor northern measures to make one beautiful whole return for display at feasts; Solene Ember was returned whole here in Y.H. 799, Y.H. 804 and Y.H. 809.
**Relations:** Inside the Bowl of Sere, about 300 leagues south of Sill; its northern interests are represented by the factor at Greybank.

### The Fenmarch
**Kind:** realm, reed confederacy. **Region:** south-west.
Peat and boardwalk villages governed by reeves chosen for standing longest in a bog; the market for cheap thin returns.
**Relations:** 90 leagues south-west of Sill; the Standing Post is its meeting place; the Empty Cantons lie between it and Grath; the Peat Line is its southern limit.

### The Standing Post
**Kind:** meeting place, village. **Region:** the Fenmarch.
A raised boardwalk and a post carved with reeves' marks where the Fen reeves settle fines and feuds.
**Relations:** About 90 leagues south-west of Sill; the only Fen place outsiders are shown.

### The Peat Line
**Kind:** border, wetland. **Region:** between the Fenmarch and Sere.
The wet frontier where Sere's northern advance stopped in Y.H. 634 and where the two powers trade, raid and buy measures.
**Relations:** Roughly 100 leagues south-west of Sill; about 200 leagues north of the Bowl of Sere.

---

## II. Grath — the birth country

### Grath
**Kind:** country, region. **Region:** north-west of the bowl.
A soft country of clay towns, peat bog and river valleys, under a Thane of Clay confirmed by the Court's factor at Greybank.
**Relations:** The Rim lies to the west and north-west; the Sallow Sea to the north-east; Sill is its principal town; Greybank its eastern gate.

### Sill
**Kind:** town. **Region:** Grath, on the Ilm.
A town of just over four thousand souls where the Ilm drops four feet over a ledge of hard stone; the birthplace of Corin.
**Relations:** On the Ilm; 18 leagues upriver (west) of Greybank; 1 league east of the Weir; 1 league north of the Old Kiln; at the meeting of the Clay Road and the river road.

### Low Kiln
**Kind:** district. **Region:** Sill, south bank below the sill.
The poor quarter of kilns, trodden-ash floors and thick grey Ash in the corners; Corin is born here and the Vail kiln-house stands here.
**Relations:** Inside Sill, on the south bank below the stone sill; High Kiln is about 200 paces up the terrace to the north; the Clay Yard is within the district, beside the Slip Brook.

Through Grain 22–24, Y.H. 813 (Days 231–233) no new place appeared in the district: the last tend of the harvest month used the same yards, rooms, roads and ways around them. On the afternoon of Grain 23 a voice with no owner, room, hand at the kiln or cause came over the boards of the Clay Yard and went off up the terrace toward \place{High Kiln} while the weather and the price of barley answered it and the row stood where it stood (T101 advanced, unpaid). A wrong wind off High Kiln brought the sweet edge of lead glaze over the boards on Grain 23 and 24 and took it out again, a direction and not a second edge paid. No collector came up or went down \place{the Clay Road} on Grain 22, 23 or 24 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 60.

Through Grain 28–30, Y.H. 813 (Days 237–239) no new place appeared in the district: the last three days of the harvest month used the same kiln-house, hearth, loaf shelf, drying racks, yard well, kindling, flagstones, wheel-shed, clay road and terrace. The year's dressed grain was turned for damp under wool, spread thin to finish and gathered back by the handful, and lay covered and unfigured near the hearth (T077); the ownerless voice came over the boards a fourth time on the afternoon of Grain 29 and went off up the terrace toward \place{High Kiln} while the row stood where it stood (T101 advanced, unpaid); a wrong wind off High Kiln brought the sweet edge of lead glaze over the boards on Grain 29 and took it out again, a direction and not a second edge paid (T064). No collector came up or went down \place{the Clay Road} on Grain 28, 29 or 30 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 62.

Through Smoke 1–3, Y.H. 813 (Days 240–242) no new place appeared in the district: the first three days of the second firing-month used the same kiln-house, hearth, kiln mouth, loaf shelf, bed, drying racks and lowest rack, yard well and bare trough, kindling, flagstones, wheel-shed, cold damper and finger's-width-open back room, Debt Loft, Clay Yard, boards, terrace and clay road. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Smoke 1, 2 or 3; Smoke 10 (Day 249) stands seven mornings ahead. The chapter's one change was an act in a place already charted: before the first firing of Smoke on the morning of Day 240 Sella Vail went to \place{the kiln mouth}, put her hand to \lore{the widow's mark}, held it one breath, lifted the clay lid with the back of a hand and tipped the carried coal under a broken pot, and the fire took, still answering four low pulls with the fifth missing and no payer named (T058/T079, T088 advanced, not paid). She did not touch the mark again at a shelf, well, rack, road, basket, afternoon or dusk on any of the three days, because the hand goes to the mark before the kiln and at no other hour. The dressed grain was turned under wool for damp, spread thin and gathered back by the handful, changed in state and given no figure (T077); a wrong wind came up off \place{High Kiln} on Smoke 3 and carried the sweet edge of \lore{lead glaze} over the boards, touched the fold and went out on the wind that brought it, a direction arrived nowhere and not a second edge paid (T064). No collector came up or went down \place{the Clay Road} on Smoke 1, 2 or 3 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 63.

Through Smoke 4–6, Y.H. 813 (Days 243–245) no new place appeared in the district: the second working week of the second firing-month used the same kiln-house, hearth, kiln mouth, loaf shelf, bed, drying racks and lowest rack, yard well and bare trough, kindling, flagstones, wheel-shed, cold damper and finger's-width-open back room, Debt Loft, Clay Yard, boards, terrace and clay road. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Smoke 4, 5 or 6; Smoke 10 (Day 249) stands four mornings ahead. At \place{the yard well} on Smoke 5 a woman of the firing work, \person[Ilva]{Ilva}, kept a count of firings notched on a short strip of birch bark in her sleeve and passed her thumb-nail across it before her eyes went to the woman who asked and before her mouth answered *Five to this morning*; the boy filed the order of reaching as a ninth record-first hand-shape on a place of its own beside the eighth, joined to none (T080 advanced, not paid), and neither read the stick nor took a figure off it. The widow's mark was touched once before the kiln on Smoke 1 and at no shelf, well, rack, road, basket, afternoon or dusk on Smoke 4, 5 or 6, because the hand goes to the mark before the kiln and at no other hour (T058/T079). The dressed grain was turned under wool for damp, spread thin and gathered back by the handful, changed in state and given no figure (T077); a wrong wind off \place{High Kiln} on Smoke 6 carried the sweet edge of \lore{lead glaze} over the boards, touched the fold and went out on the wind that brought it, a direction arrived nowhere and not a second edge paid (T064). No collector came up or went down \place{the Clay Road} on Smoke 4, 5 or 6 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 64.

Through Smoke 7–9, Y.H. 813 (Days 246–248) no new place appeared in the district: the last working week of the second firing-month used the same kiln-house, hearth, kiln mouth, loaf shelf, bed, drying racks and lowest rack, yard well and bare trough, kindling, flagstones, wheel-shed, cold damper and finger's-width-open back room, Debt Loft, Clay Yard, boards, terrace and clay road. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Smoke 7, 8 or 9; Smoke 10 (Day 249), the first Account-day and market of the second firing, stood one morning beyond the close and was not brought early. On the afternoon of Smoke 8 a voice with no owner, room, hand at the kiln or cause came over \place{the boards} a fifth time and went off up \place{the terrace} toward \place{High Kiln} while the weather and the price of barley answered it and the row stood where it stood; five askings are still not one witness and no row was named (T101 advanced, unpaid). A wrong wind off High Kiln carried the sweet edge of \lore{lead glaze} over the boards, touched the fold of dressed grain and went out on the wind that brought it, a direction arrived nowhere and not a second edge paid (T064). At \place{the yard well} on Smoke 8 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was seen again drawing water with her hand in her sleeve; she answered the question put and not the one behind it, and no figure came off her strip (T080 held). The widow's mark was touched once before the kiln on Smoke 1 and at no shelf, well, rack, road, basket, afternoon or dusk on Smoke 7, 8 or 9, because the hand goes to the mark before the kiln and at no other hour (T058/T079). The dressed grain was turned under wool, spread thin and gathered by the handful, changed in state and given no figure (T077); no collector came up or went down \place{the Clay Road} on Smoke 7, 8 or 9 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 65.

Through Smoke 10–12, Y.H. 813 (Days 249–251) no new place appeared in the district: the first Account-day and market of the second firing and the two ordinary tending days used the same kiln-house, hearth, kiln mouth, loaf shelf, bed, drying racks and lowest rack, yard well and bare trough, kindling, flagstones, wheel-shed, cold damper and finger's-width-open back room, Debt Loft, Clay Yard, boards, terrace, clay road, Cross, reading block under the moot-post, pot board and out-of-line landing below the north bridge. Smoke 10 was the first Account-day and market of the second firing; Sella set the basket in the moot-post's finger's width of shade where no slate lay and stood half a pace off through Berr Clay's twice-read figure and the bell, and carried him home at the going of the light saying nothing of what had been read over him. Berr read *one in twelve, as the notice says* twice over the basket, called the levy, denied it with *your house*, never *I*, and went off up \place{the clay road} toward \place{High Kiln} with the box locked and the true subtraction inside; the district's arithmetic changed for the first time in Smoke and could not be changed back, while the printed figure stood unchallenged and the figure read was still not the figure paid. The bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself, the heard bell kept beside the told; Hessa Rale carried the slate up the road toward \place{Greybank} edge-on and unread, and the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the out-of-line landing for a measure nobody called (T078, T080, T090). There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Smoke 11 and 12; the dressed grain lay covered and unfigured under wool, read over on Smoke 10 by a number that did not know what it cost, and the space between that figure and its price had no signature (T077). At the yard well on Smoke 11 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was seen with her hand in her sleeve and answered *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account*, so no figure came off her strip (T080 held). The widow's mark was touched once before the kiln on Smoke 1 and at no shelf, well, rack, road, basket, afternoon or dusk on Smoke 10, 11 or 12, because the hand goes to the mark before the kiln and at no other hour (T058/T079); no collector came up or went down \place{the Clay Road} on any of the three days (T009/T013/T092). Smoke 20 (Day 259), the second Account-day and market of the second firing, stood eight mornings beyond the close and was not brought early. Ch 66.

Through Smoke 13–15, Y.H. 813 (Days 252–254) no new place appeared in the district: the middle tend of the second firing-month used the same kiln-house, hearth, kiln mouth, loaf shelf, bed, boards, drying racks and lowest rack, yard well and bare trough, kindling, flagstones, wheel-shed, cold damper and finger's-width-open back room, Debt Loft, Clay Yard, terrace and clay road. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Smoke 13, 14 or 15; Smoke 20 (Day 259), the second Account-day and market of the second firing, stood five mornings beyond the close and was not brought early. The dressed grain was turned under wool, spread thin, gathered by the handful and laid flat, changed in state and given no figure (T077); the widow's mark was touched once before the kiln on Smoke 1 and at no shelf, well, rack, road, basket, afternoon or dusk on Smoke 13, 14 or 15 (T058/T079). On Smoke 14 a pot set down wrong at the drying racks, out of a woman's shadow and near the warm side of the wall, gave its high thin note before its time and was swept around with a practical word about the slip; no pot was lost, no fourth likeness was made a cause and no name was given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188 (T064). A wrong wind off \place{High Kiln} brought the sweet edge of \lore{lead glaze} over the boards, touched the fold and went out on the wind that brought it, a direction arrived nowhere and not a second edge paid. The ownerless question over the boards was not asked a sixth time and stood at five askings with no witness and no row named (T101 held). At the yard well on Smoke 13 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} answered *I keep the count, not the account* with her hand in her sleeve; no figure came off her strip (T080 held). No collector came up or went down \place{the Clay Road} on Smoke 13, 14 or 15 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 67.

Through Smoke 16–18, Y.H. 813 (Days 255–257) no new place appeared in the district: the middle tend of the second firing-month used the same kiln-house, hearth, kiln mouth, loaf shelf and bed, the drying racks and lowest rack, the rack end where the dressed grain was covered under wool away from the hearth, the yard well with its stone lip and bare trough, the kindling, flagstones, wheel-shed, cold damper and finger's-width-open back room, Debt Loft, Clay Yard, boards, terrace and clay road. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Smoke 16, 17 or 18; Smoke 20 (Day 259), the second Account-day and market, stood two mornings beyond the close and was not brought early. On Smoke 17 the dressed grain was gathered in by the handful for the last time before Smoke's second reading, covered under wool and set away from the hearth on the cool stone at the rack end, changed in state and given no figure (T077 advanced, unpaid). On Smoke 18 frost-bloom wrote white in fronds on the stone lip of the yard well where the Warm had lately gone out; the rope and bucket knocked it grey and the yard called it weather, and the boy kept the white lip on a place of its own with no cause — not a payer, not the widow's mark (a well is not an opening) and not the first firing of Frost (T083/T085 carried). At the well on Smoke 16 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} answered a question about the firings with her hand in her sleeve and her strip unread (T080 held). The widow's mark was touched once before the kiln on Smoke 1 and at no shelf, well, rack, road, basket, afternoon or dusk on Smoke 16, 17 or 18 (T058/T079). No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Smoke 16, 17 or 18 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 68.

Through Smoke 19–21, Y.H. 813 (Days 258–260) no new place appeared in the district: the second Account-day and the third working week used the same kiln-house, hearth, kiln mouth, loaf shelf and bed, the drying racks and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, the boards, the yard well with \place{the trough} bare and dark, the kindling and flagstones, the wheel-shed, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open back room, the Debt Loft, the Clay Yard, the terrace, the clay road, the Cross, the reading block under \place{the moot-post}, the pot board and the out-of-line landing below \place{the north bridge}. Smoke 20 (Day 259) was the second Account-day and market of the second firing: Berr Clay read *one in twelve, as the notice says* twice over the basket, called the levy, denied it with *your house*, never *I*, and went off up \place{the clay road} with the box locked and the true subtraction inside; the district's arithmetic changed a second time in Smoke and cannot be changed back, while the printed figure stood unchallenged and the figure read was still not the figure paid, and the figure read over the dressed grain covered at home did not know what it cost. Sella set the basket in the moot-post's finger's width of shade where no slate lay and stood half a pace off through the reading and the bell, and carried him home at the going of the light saying nothing. The bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself, Hessa Rale took the slate up the road toward \place{Greybank} unread, the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the out-of-line landing for a measure nobody called, and at the pot board and the market's edge a silent woman's green and a foundling's shape stayed sightings with no eighth of either. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Smoke 19 and 21; the dressed grain was lifted for damp and turned under wool on those two mornings and given no figure, frost went out of the well lip under the bucket and the yard called it weather, and the widow's mark was touched once before the kiln on Smoke 1 and at no shelf, well, rack, road, basket, market, morning, afternoon, dusk or going of the light on Smoke 19, 20 or 21 (T058/T079, T077, T083/T085). At the well on Smoke 19 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} answered *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account* with her hand in her sleeve and her strip unread (T080 held). No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Smoke 19, 20 or 21 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 69.

Through Smoke 22–24, Y.H. 813 (Days 261–263) no new place appeared in the district: the third working week of the second firing-month used the same kiln-house, hearth, kiln mouth, loaf shelf and bed, the drying racks and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, the boards, the yard well with its stone lip and bare trough, the kindling and flagstones, the wheel-shed, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open back room, the Debt Loft, the Clay Yard, the terrace and the clay road. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Smoke 22, 23 or 24; Smoke 30 (Day 269), the third and last Account-day of the second firing, stood six mornings ahead and was not brought early. The chapter's one change was made in places already charted: the boy registered that the house keeps itself by \lore{not-doings} --- the two unswept lines at \place{the boards}, the widow's mark untouched, the charcoal on the underside of \place{the loaf shelf} unread, the fifth pull of \place{the carried fire} unsupplied under the floor, Garl's loan uncalled at \place{the kindling}, no number spoken over the basket and the crust in the fold not lifted --- and kept the seeing as a finding with no payer (Ch 70). At \place{the yard well} on Smoke 23 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was seen once drawing with the pail against her knee, clay pale in her nail crescents and rack dust in one thumb seam, her hand in her sleeve and the notched strip unread, answering *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account* (T080 held at nine); on Smoke 22 and 24 she was offstage. Frost went out of the well lip under the bucket and the yard called it weather; a wrong wind off \place{High Kiln} brought the sweet edge of \lore{lead glaze} over \place{the boards}, touched the fold and went out on the wind that brought it, a direction arrived nowhere (T064). No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Smoke 22, 23 or 24 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 70.

Through Smoke 25–27, Y.H. 813 (Days 264–266) no new place appeared in the district: the third working week of the second firing-month used the same kiln-house, hearth, kiln mouth, loaf shelf and bed, the boards fronting the shelf, the drying racks and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay under wool, the yard well with its stone lip and bare trough, the kindling and flagstones, the wheel-shed, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open back room, the Debt Loft, the Clay Yard, the terrace and the clay road. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Smoke 25, 26 or 27; Smoke 30 (Day 269), the third and last Account-day of the second firing, stood three mornings ahead and was not brought early, and the first firing of Frost (Day 271) stood far off. The dressed grain was lifted at a corner for damp on the 25th, drawn wholly out of the wool and turned piece by piece in the flat of a hand for damp and rot on the 26th, with each piece set down where it lay and no figure given, and turned once with a lighter touch under the wool on the 27th; the arithmetic on Smoke 30 will still not know what it cost and the space between the figure and its price stays unsigned (T077). At \place{the yard well} on Smoke 26 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was glimpsed once drawing with the pail against her knee, clay pale in her nail crescents and rack dust in one thumb seam, her hand in her sleeve with the notched birch strip unread, answering *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account*; on the 25th and 27th she was offstage (T080 held at nine). Frost that had written white on the north stone lip of the well was grey under the bucket on the 25th and gone by the 26th and 27th, and the yard called the whole of it weather (T083/T085). On Smoke 26 and 27 a wrong wind off \place{High Kiln} carried the sweet edge of \lore{lead glaze} over \place{the boards}, touched the fold and went out on the wind that brought it, a direction arrived nowhere and not a second edge paid (T064). Mid-afternoon on Smoke 26 a voice with no owner reported over the boards that somebody had been asking which row went quiet; the weather and the price of barley answered it and it went off up \place{the terrace}, a further shaping of the row question and not a sixth asking, with no witness and no row named (T101 advanced, unpaid). No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Smoke 25, 26 or 27 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 71.

Through Smoke 28–30, Y.H. 813 (Days 267–269) no new place appeared in the district: the last tend of the second firing-month used the same kiln-house, hearth, kiln mouth, loaf shelf and bed, the boards fronting the shelf, the drying racks and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, the yard well with its stone lip and bare trough, the kindling and flagstones, the wheel-shed, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open back room, the Debt Loft, the Clay Yard, the terrace and the clay road. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Smoke 28 and 29; Smoke 30 (Day 269) was the third and last Account-day and market of the second firing, earned by its own morning, and went out without a boundary drawn while neither of the district's two names for the months was corrected. Frost came on in the dark beyond the door and was not brought into a day that had not earned it; the first firing of Frost (Day 271) stood far off. The dressed grain was turned once for damp under wool on the 28th and once with a lighter touch on the 29th and lay covered and unfigured on the cool stone at the rack end on the 30th, read over by Smoke's third arithmetic and not knowing its cost, the space between the figure and its price unsigned (T077). At \place{the yard well} on Smoke 29 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was glimpsed once drawing with the pail against her knee, clay dried pale in her nail crescents and grey rack dust in one thumb seam, her hand going toward her sleeve and staying there with the notched strip unread, answering *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account*; on the 28th and 30th she was offstage (T080 held at nine). Three or more men went low into \place{the back room} before first grey on the mornings of Days 267, 268 and 269 and got no name from \person{Jorra Vail}, who came through with meal-fine grey slate dust past the bent finger and put that finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence (T035/T031, T062). At \place{the kindling} Tarn kept six counted where the house could check and the seventh at the very lip where it could not, the ear-sentence stopped short of the calling word as a remembered ninth stopping with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled (T036/T076). No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Smoke 28, 29 or 30 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 72.

Through Frost 1–3, Y.H. 813 (Days 270–272) no new place appeared in the district: the first three days of the ninth month used the same kiln-house, hearth, kiln mouth, loaf shelf, bed, boards, drying racks and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, the yard well and bare trough, the kindling and flagstones, the wheel-shed, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open back room, the Debt Loft, the Clay Yard, the terrace and the clay road. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Frost 1, 2 or 3; Frost 10 (Day 279), the month's first Account-day and market, stands six mornings ahead and was not brought early. Frost came on without a boundary drawn and neither of the district's two names for the months was corrected. The chapter's one change was made in a place already charted: before the first firing of Frost on the morning of Day 271 \person{Sella Vail} went to \place{the kiln mouth}, put her hand to \lore{the widow's mark}, held it one breath, let go, lifted the clay lid with the back of a hand and tipped the carried coal under the back of a broken pot, and the fire took, still answering four low pulls with the silence where the fifth should have stood and its payer unnamed for two hundred and seventy-one days; nobody praised it, named what it mended, entered a slate or made a lesson of it, and the next opening stands in a later month's first firing, far off (T058/T079, T088 advanced, not paid). The mark was touched at no shelf, well, rack, road, basket, yard, morning, hearth, afternoon, dusk or going of the light on Frost 1, 2 or 3, because the hand goes to the mark before the kiln and at no other hour. The dressed grain was turned beneath the wool for damp with a progressively lighter touch and given no figure; Smoke's third arithmetic stayed unread up \place{the clay road} in Berr Clay's locked box and the figure read was still not the figure paid (T077). At \place{the yard well} on Frost 1 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was seen once drawing with the pail against her knee, clay dried pale in her nail crescents and rack dust in one thumb seam, her hand toward her sleeve and staying there, answering *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account*; the ninth hand stayed nine (T080 held). Three or more men went low into \place{the back room} before first grey and got no name from \person{Jorra Vail}; the burned slate stood at four, the shut-room tellings at two and the row question at five askings with no witness (T035/T031, T065/T095, T101 held). No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Frost 1, 2 or 3 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 73.

Through Frost 4–6, Y.H. 813 (Days 273–275) no new place appeared in the district: the first working week used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, hearth, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, the \place{yard well} and bare \place{trough}, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the terrace} and \place{the clay road}. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Frost 4, 5 or 6; Frost 10 (Day 279), the month's first Account-day and market, stands four mornings ahead and was not brought early. The week's one change was made in a place already charted: on Frost 5 (Day 274) \person{Sella Vail}, standing with her back to the basket at \place{the loaf shelf}, put her finger BENEATH the shelf's front edge where the charcoal tally lies and held it for two counted breaths where on Day 166 it had stayed for one, then brought it away without a look, a word or a going back; the charcoal was not read, copied or joined to the two gaps above, and the boy filed the returned held beat on the shelf's own place beside the Day 161 fraction at \lore{the damper} and the Tallow 22 flagstone half-check (T100 advanced, not paid). The dressed grain was turned under wool for damp with a progressively lighter touch and given no figure (T077); at \place{the yard well} on Frost 5 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was glimpsed once with her hand to her sleeve and her strip unread, answering *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account* (T080 held at nine). A wrong wind off \place{High Kiln} brought the sweet edge of \lore{lead glaze} over \place{the boards}, touched the fold and went out on the wind that brought it, a direction arrived nowhere (T064). Seven walked-around places were gone around with a look and a wide step and no number, including the returning-line place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out (T098 carried). No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Frost 4, 5 or 6 (T009/T013/T092). No new place is wanted on a map. Ch 74.

Through Frost 7–9, Y.H. 813 (Days 276–278) no new place appeared in the district: the last working week used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, hearth, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, the \place{yard well} and its stone lip, the \place{trough} beside it, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the terrace} and \place{the clay road}. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Frost 7, 8 or 9; Frost 10 (Day 279), the month's first Account-day and market, stood one morning beyond the close and was not brought into a day that had not earned it. The chapter's one change was made in places already charted: on Frost 8 the white \lore{frost-bloom} on the north lip of \place{the yard well} did not go with the sun as it had gone on Frost 4 but stayed past noon, past the going of the light and onto Frost 9; the rope at the well stiffened, the water in \place{the trough} skinned over from edge to edge, and the coals were banked an hour earlier at the going of the light, and the yard called all of it weather and gave nobody a figure. On Frost 9 \person{Sella Vail} broke the skin with the back of the broken pot she turns the coals with, lifted a plate of ice clouded with silt and set it on a flat stone beside the well where no foot went, out of the path of feet, and drew what the yard calls weather, telling the weather where to stand (T083/T085 advanced, not paid). No new room, no new road and no new landmark: the flat stone by the well is the stone already charted beside \place{the trough}, and the plate of ice is an object set upon it, not a place. The dressed grain was turned a second time on Frost 9 because the stone at the rack end had gone cold, and still lay covered and unfigured, so the arithmetic that will read over it tomorrow will not know what it cost (T077). Smoke's third arithmetic stayed unread up \place{the clay road} in Berr Clay's locked box; at \place{the yard well} on Frost 8 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was glimpsed once with her hand toward her sleeve and her strip unread, answering *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account* (T080 held at nine). Seven walked-around places were gone around with a look and a wide step and no number, and no collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Frost 7, 8 or 9 (T009/T013/T092). No map is owed. Ch 75.

Through Frost 10–12, Y.H. 813 (Days 279–281) no new place appeared in the district: Frost's first Account-day and market used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, hearth, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, the \place{yard well} with its stone lip, the \place{trough} and the flat stone beside it, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the terrace}, \place{the clay road}, \place{the Cross}, \place{the reading block under the moot-post}, \place{the pot board} and \place{the out-of-line landing} below \place{the north bridge}. Frost 10 was the month's first Account-day and market: Berr Clay read *one in twelve, as the notice says* at the reading block, called the levy and denied it with *your house*, never *I*, and went off up the clay road with the box locked and the first true subtraction of Frost inside; the district's arithmetic changed for the month and cannot be changed back, while the printed figure stood unchallenged, the figure read was still not the figure paid, and the reading answered nothing for the man carried out on Day 188, the hand that went in on Seed 15 or the dressed grain covered under wool at the rack end, read over by a number that did not know what it cost (T015/T041/T047, T077, T098 carried). Sella set the basket in the moot-post's finger's width of shade where no slate lay and stood half a pace off through the reading and the bell, carrying him home at the going of the light saying nothing (T058/T079); Hessa Rale carried the slate up the road toward \place{Greybank} before noon, edge-on and unread (T080); the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the out-of-line landing for a measure nobody called (T090); the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself, heard beside told (T078); at the pot board and the market's edge a silent woman's green and a foundling's shape stayed sightings with no eighth of either (T050/T051, T033/T034 held). There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Frost 11 and 12; the dressed grain was turned for damp under wool with a lighter touch on those two mornings and given no figure (T077); at \place{the yard well} on Frost 11 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was glimpsed once drawing with the pail against her knee, her hand going toward her sleeve and staying there while she answered *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account*, no figure coming off her strip (T080 held at nine). The stayed frost on the well lip and the plate of ice on its flat stone were still called weather and entered nowhere, and the reading took no figure off either (T083/T085 held, not paid). No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Frost 10, 11 or 12 (T009/T013/T092). Frost 20 (Day 289), the month's second Account-day, stood eight mornings ahead and was not brought into a day that had not earned it. Ch 76.

Through Frost 13–15, Y.H. 813 (Days 282–284) no new place appeared in the district: the second working week used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, hearth, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, the \place{yard well} with its stone lip, the skinned \place{trough} and the \place{flat stone by the well}, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the terrace} and \place{the clay road}. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Frost 13, 14 or 15; Frost 20 (Day 289), the month's second Account-day, stood five mornings beyond the close and was not brought into a day that had not earned it. The dressed grain was turned under wool for damp on all three mornings with a progressively lighter touch and given no figure (T077); the stayed frost on the well lip, the skinned trough and the plate of ice on its flat stone were still called weather and entered nowhere, and no figure, sign, cause or payer was taken off any of them (T083/T085 held, not paid). At the well on Frost 14 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was glimpsed once with her strip unread, answering *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account* (T080 held at nine), and another woman waiting her turn spoke of the runner who once carried a figure that named a person she knew; the saying was kept on a place of its own beside \person[Hessa Rale]'s eighth hand with a finger between, not a witness, not a confession, not a joining and not paid (T080 advanced, not paid). Three or more men went low into \place{the back room} before first grey on the mornings of Days 282, 283 and 284 and got no name from \person{Jorra Vail}; the burned slate stood at four, the shut-room tellings at two and the row question at five askings with no witness (T035/T031, T065/T095, T101 held). At \place{the kindling} Tarn kept six counted where the house could check and the seventh at the lip where it could not, the ear-sentence stopped at the calling word as a remembered ninth stopping with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled (T036/T076). The widow's mark hung where it hung at a morning, an afternoon and a going of the light, because none was an opening (T058/T079). No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Frost 13, 14 or 15 (T009/T013/T092). No new room, no new road and no new landmark; no map is owed. Ch 77.

Through Frost 16–18, Y.H. 813 (Days 285–287) no new place appeared in the district: the third working week used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, hearth, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, the \place{yard well} with its stone lip, the skinned \place{trough} and the \place{flat stone by the well}, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the terrace} and \place{the clay road}. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Frost 16, 17 or 18; Frost 20 (Day 289), the month's second Account-day and market, stood two mornings beyond the close and was not brought into a day that had not earned it. The stayed frost on the well lip, the skinned trough and the plate of ice clouded with silt on its flat stone were still called weather and entered nowhere (T083/T085 held, not paid). At \place{the yard well} on Frost 17 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was glimpsed once drawing with the pail against her knee, clay pale in her nail crescents and rack dust in one thumb seam, her hand going toward her sleeve and staying there, answering *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account*; on the 16th and 18th she was offstage and the notches stayed unread (T080 held at nine). Three or more men went low into \place{the back room} before first grey on the mornings of Days 285, 286 and 287 and got no name from \person{Jorra Vail}; the burned slate stood at four, the shut-room tellings at two and the row question at five askings with no witness (T035/T031, T065/T095, T101 held). At \place{the kindling} Tarn kept six counted where the house could check and the seventh at the lip where it could not, the ear-sentence stopped at the calling word as a remembered ninth stopping with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled (T036/T076). The widow's mark hung where it hung at a morning, an afternoon and a going of the light, because none was an opening (T058/T079); the Frost 14 saying about the runner held with no second shaping on these three days. No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Frost 16, 17 or 18 (T009/T013/T092). No new room, no new road and no new landmark; no map is owed. Ch 78.

Through Frost 19–21, Y.H. 813 (Days 288–290) no new place appeared in the district: the fourth working week used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, hearth, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, the \place{yard well} with its stone lip, the skinned \place{trough} and the \place{flat stone by the well}, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the terrace} and \place{the clay road}. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Frost 19 or 21; Frost 20 (Day 289), the month's second Account-day and market, brought the \place{Cross}, \place{the reading block under the moot-post} with its finger's width of thin shade, \place{the pot board}, \place{the out-of-line landing} below \place{the north bridge}, the clay road up toward \place{High Kiln} and on to \place{Greybank}, and the \place{terrace} back on stage — all ground already drawn on \place{atlas/home-settlement}, \place{atlas/the-clay-yard}, \place{atlas/cross-and-fishstairs}, \place{atlas/vail-house}, \place{atlas/high-kiln} and \place{atlas/slate-row}. Berr Clay read *one in twelve, as the notice says* twice over the basket there, once for the moot and once for the households that came late, and went off up the clay road with the box locked and Frost's second true subtraction inside; Sella set the basket in the moot-post's thin shade where no slate lay and stood half a pace off through the reading and the bell, carrying him home at the going of the light saying nothing (T015/T041/T047, T058/T079); Hessa Rale carried the slate up the road toward \place{Greybank} before noon, edge-on and unread (T080); the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the out-of-line landing for a measure nobody called (T090); the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself (T078); at the pot board and the market's edge a silent woman's green and a foundling's shape stayed sightings with no eighth of either (T050/T051, T033/T034 held). The stayed frost on the well lip, the skinned trough and the plate of ice clouded with silt on its flat stone were still called weather and entered nowhere (T083/T085 held, not paid). On Frost 19 an ownerless voice over the boards and the well-roof gave the well-saying about the runner a second shaping — what a runner carries in a slate that has a name in it — and the boy filed it on a place of its own beside the first and \person[Hessa Rale]'s eighth hand with a finger between, not a witness, not a cause, not a confession and not paid (T080 advanced, not paid); it is a voice arrived nowhere and not a feature of the ground. At \place{the kindling} Tarn kept six counted where the house could check and the seventh at the lip where it could not, the ear-sentence stopped at the calling word as a remembered ninth stopping with no tenth, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled (T036/T076). Three or more men went low into \place{the back room} before first grey on the mornings of Days 288, 289 and 290 and got no name from \person{Jorra Vail}; the burned slate stood at four, the shut-room tellings at two and the row question at five askings with no witness (T035/T031, T065/T095, T101 held). The dressed grain was turned beneath the wool with a progressively lighter touch and given no figure (T077); the widow's mark hung where it hung at a morning, an afternoon and a going of the light, because none was an opening (T058/T079). No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Frost 19, 20 or 21 (T009/T013/T092). Frost 30 (Day 299), the month's third and last Account-day, stands nine mornings ahead and was not brought into a day that had not earned it. No new room, no new road and no new landmark; no map is owed. Ch 79.

Through Frost 22–24, Y.H. 813 (Days 291–293) no new place appeared in the district: the last working week used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, hearth, \place{the kiln mouth}, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the towel}, \place{the pot board}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, \place{the yard well} with its stone lip, the skinned \place{trough} and the \place{flat stone by the well}, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the terrace} and \place{the clay road}. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Frost 22, 23 or 24, and Frost 30 (Day 299), the month's third and last Account-day and market, stood six mornings ahead and was not brought into a day that had not earned it. On the afternoon of Frost 23 an ownerless voice over \place{the boards} and the well-roof gave the Frost 14 well-saying a third shaping — the slate-runner once carried a name the district spent and did not say whose — with nobody standing anywhere to be asked; it gave no new room, road or landmark, and the boy did not make three shapings stand up as a witness (T080 advanced, not paid). Sella carried him down and up at the half-pace from every open slate and went wide around the seven walked-around places with a look and a wide step and no number: the clean dark line and the finer grey called the thaw, the space beneath the lowest rack, the gone pan's round ring, the skinned trough, the field's empty place and the returning-line place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried). The stayed frost on the well lip and the clouded plate of ice on its flat stone stayed weather entered nowhere, with no fourth likeness made a cause (T083/T085 held). At \place{the kindling} Tarn kept six checkable and the seventh at the lip beside Garl's unforged collector sentence, stopped the ear-sentence at the calling word as a remembered ninth stopping with no tenth, and kept the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled (T036/T076); at the shed edge Jorra came through with meal-fine grey slate dust past the bent finger, spoke to the wheel and a pot before any person and put that finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence (T035/T031/T062). Garl stayed in objects with his name unspoken two hundred and ninety-one through two hundred and ninety-three days, and no collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Frost 22, 23 or 24 (T009/T013/T092). The widow's mark hung where it hung at a morning and a going of the light, because neither is an opening (T058/T079). The dressed grain was turned beneath the wool with a progressively lighter touch and given no figure, and Frost's first and second arithmetics stayed locked in Berr's box up the road with the figure read still not the figure paid (T077, T015/T041/T047). No new room, no new road and no new landmark; no map is owed. Ch 80.

Through Frost 25–27, Y.H. 813 (Days 294–296) no new place appeared in the district: Frost's last tend used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, hearth, \place{the kiln mouth}, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the towel}, \place{the pot board}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, \place{the yard well} with its stone lip, the skinned \place{trough} and the \place{flat stone by the well}, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the terrace} and \place{the clay road}. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Frost 25, 26 or 27, and Frost 30 (Day 299), the month's third and last Account-day and market, stood three mornings ahead and was not brought into a day that had not earned it. At \place{the yard well} on the afternoon of Frost 26 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was glimpsed once drawing with the pail against her knee, clay pale in her nail crescents and rack dust in one thumb seam; a slate lay open on the stone lip with the writing turned toward its owner and nobody reading it aloud, her hand went toward her sleeve and stayed there, and the notched strip was not taken out, read, copied, counted or joined to the slate and no figure came off the wood. She answered the question put and not the one behind it, *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account*, and went back to her pail; the boy filed the order of reaching as the ninth record-first hand with no tenth and kept the slate's nearness as an absence (T080 advanced, not paid). On Frost 25 and 27 she was offstage. Sella carried him down and up at the half-pace from every open slate and went wide around the seven walked-around places with a look and a wide step and no number: the clean dark line and the finer grey called the thaw, the space beneath the lowest rack, the gone pan's round ring, the skinned trough, the field's empty place and the returning-line place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried). The stayed frost on the well lip and the clouded plate of ice on its flat stone stayed weather entered nowhere, with no fourth likeness made a cause (T083/T085 held). At \place{the kindling} Tarn kept six where the house could check and the seventh at the lip beside Garl's unforged collector sentence, stopped the ear-sentence at the calling word as a remembered ninth stopping with no tenth, and kept the Seed 16 breath unspent, the warm stone cold and the loan uncalled; at the wheel-shed edge Jorra's meal-fine grey dust, cold damper and withheld note stood as before, with the burned slate at four, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings and *he's not Vail* sealed. Garl stayed in objects with his name unspoken two hundred and ninety-four through two hundred and ninety-six days and no collector up or down the clay road (T009/T013/T092). Frost went out without a boundary drawn and neither of the district's two habits of naming its months was set right or left wrong. Ch 81.

Through Frost 28–30, Y.H. 813 (Days 297–299) no new place appeared in the district: Frost's last three days used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, hearth, \place{the kiln mouth}, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the towel}, \place{the pot board}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, \place{the yard well} with its stone lip, the skinned \place{trough} and the \place{flat stone by the well}, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the terrace} and \place{the clay road}. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Frost 28 or 29; Frost 30 (Day 299), the month's third and last Account-day and market, brought the \place{Cross}, \place{the reading block under the moot-post} with its finger's width of thin shade, \place{the pot board}, \place{the out-of-line landing} below \place{the north bridge}, and the clay road up toward \place{High Kiln} and on to \place{Greybank} back on stage — all ground already drawn on \place{atlas/home-settlement}, \place{atlas/the-clay-yard}, \place{atlas/cross-and-fishstairs}, \place{atlas/vail-house}, \place{atlas/high-kiln} and \place{atlas/slate-row}. Frost's third arithmetic went up \place{the clay road} in \person{Berr Clay}'s locked box behind the first two with one in twelve printed and unchallenged and the figure read still not the figure paid and no signature on the space between; the district's arithmetic changed a third time for the month and could not be changed back, and the dressed grain covered under wool at \place{the rack end} was read over a third time by a number that did not know its cost (T015/T041/T047 advanced, not paid; T077). Sella set the basket in the moot-post's finger's width of shade where no slate lay and stood half a pace off through the reading and the bell, carrying him home at the going of the light saying nothing (T058/T079); the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself (T078); \person{Hessa Rale} carried the slate up the road toward \place{Greybank} before noon, edge-on and unread (T080); the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at \place{the out-of-line landing} for a measure nobody called (T090); at \place{the pot board} and the market's edge a silent woman's green stayed a colour and a foundling's shape a shape, with no eighth of either (T050/T051, T033/T034 held). At \place{the yard well} on Frost 28 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was glimpsed once drawing with the pail against her knee, her hand going to her sleeve and staying there with the notched strip unread; on Frost 29 and 30 she was offstage and no figure came off the wood (T080 advanced, not paid). The stayed frost on the well lip, the skinned trough and the silt-clouded plate of ice on its flat stone stayed weather entered nowhere (T083/T085 held). Three or more men unnamed went low into \place{the back room} before first grey and got no name from \person{Jorra Vail}; the burned slate stood at four, the shut-room tellings at two and the row question at five askings with no witness. At \place{the kindling} Tarn kept six counted where the house could check and the seventh at the lip beside Garl's unforged collector sentence, the ear-sentence stopped at the calling word as a remembered ninth stopping with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, the loan uncalled and no collector up or down \place{the clay road} (T036/T076). No new room, road or landmark came into the district; Frost went out without a boundary drawn and neither of the district's two habits of naming its months was set right or left wrong (T083/T085). Ch 82.

Through Bone 1–3, Y.H. 813 (Days 300–302) no new place appeared in the district: the first three days of the hunting-and-repair month used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, hearth, \place{the kiln mouth}, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the towel}, \place{the pot board}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, \place{the yard well} with its stone lip, the skinned \place{trough} and the \place{flat stone by the well}, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the worked strips}, \place{the terrace} and \place{the clay road}. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Bone 1, 2 or 3; Bone 10 (Day 309), the month's first Account-day and market, stood seven mornings ahead and was not brought early. Bone came on without a boundary drawn and neither of the district's two habits of naming its months was set right or left wrong. On Bone 2, before the basket, \person{Sella Vail} went to \place{the kiln mouth}, held \lore{the widow's mark} one breath and tipped the carried coal under a broken pot; the fire took in fours with its fifth unpaid three hundred and one days then three hundred and two, nobody praised the opening, named what it mended or made a lesson of it, and five openings in five months were five and not a rule (T058/T079, T088 advanced, not paid). On Bone 1 a hunter come down from \place{Cold Acre} waited at \place{the yard well} with a hare across his shoulders, judged the carrying arm and not by a figure, answered a question about its cost with the weather and went off toward \place{the north gate road} — both already charted on \place{atlas/cold-acre} and \place{atlas/bone-orchard}, and no new room, road or landmark came into the district (T077 advanced, not paid). At \place{the yard well} on Bone 2 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} answered a question about the month's firings with her hand toward her sleeve and the notched strip unread, the ninth hand nine and no figure coming off the wood (T080 held). On Bone 3 the stayed \lore{frost-bloom} went off the well's north lip while the trough kept its skin and the silt-clouded plate of ice stood on its flat stone out of the path of feet, all called weather and entered nowhere (T083/T085 held). Three or more men went low into \place{the back room} before first grey on the mornings of Days 300, 301 and 302 and got no name from \person{Jorra Vail}; the burned slate stood at four, the shut-room tellings at two and the row question at five askings with no witness. No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Bone 1, 2 or 3 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 83.

Through Bone 4–6, Y.H. 813 (Days 303–305) no new place appeared in the district: the second and third working weeks of the hunting-and-repair month used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, \place{the hearth}, \place{the pot board} with the twice-mended pot, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the towel}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, \place{the yard well} with its stone lip, the skinned \place{trough} and the \place{flat stone by the well}, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the worked strips}, \place{the terrace} and \place{the clay road}. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Bone 4, 5 or 6; Bone 10 (Day 309), the month's first Account-day and market, stood four mornings ahead and was not brought early. Bone went on without a boundary drawn and neither of the district's two habits of naming its months was corrected. On Bone 5 an ownerless voice came up from \place{Slate Row} over \place{the boards} of \place{the Clay Yard}, on the same breath as the price of barley and the weather, and reported that a slate had burned again; no owner stood under it, no room, no hand at the flame, no reason and no witness, and the boy filed the **fifth telling of the burned slate** beside the four with a finger between, still not a third telling of the counting room shut in the war and no sixth due (T035/T031/T065/T095 advanced, not paid). The voice went off up \place{the terrace} toward \place{High Kiln}, both already charted, and no new road or landmark came into the district. On Bone 4 men come down from \place{Cold Acre} carried hares across their shoulders, judged by the carrying arm and the weather and entered nowhere (T077); the stayed frost had gone off the well's north lip while the trough kept its skin and the clouded plate of ice stood on its flat stone out of the path of feet, all called weather (T083/T085 held). On Bone 5 and 6 \person{Sella Vail} lifted the twice-mended pot on \place{the pot board} with her thumb first where the wire crossed the crack and said both proverbs to the pot and the fire; the crack left showing was kept as \person{Garl Vail}'s saying and not made a doctrine (T009/T013/T092, T058/T079). At \place{the yard well} on Bone 6 \person{Ilva} answered a question about the month's firings with her hand toward her sleeve and the notched strip unread, the ninth hand nine and no figure coming off the wood (T080 advanced, not paid). Three or more men went low into \place{the back room} before first grey on the mornings of Days 303, 304 and 305 and got no name from \person{Jorra Vail}; the shut-room tellings stayed at two, the row question at five askings with no witness. No collector came up or went down \place{the clay road} on Bone 4, 5 or 6 (T009/T013/T092). Ch 84.

Through Bone 7–9, Y.H. 813 (Days 306–308) no new place appeared in the district: the last tend of the hunting-and-repair month used the same \place{the Vail kiln-house}, \place{the hearth}, \place{the pot board} with the twice-mended pot, \place{the loaf shelf}, \place{the bed}, \place{the boards}, \place{the towel}, \place{the drying racks} and the cool stone at \place{the rack end} where the dressed grain lay covered under wool, \place{the yard well} with its stone lip, the skinned \place{trough} and the \place{flat stone by the well}, \place{the kindling} and \place{the flagstones}, \place{the Vail wheel-shed}, \lore{the damper} and the finger's-width-open \place{the back room}, \place{the Debt Loft}, \place{the Clay Yard}, \place{the worked strips}, \place{the terrace} and \place{the clay road}. There was no market, no Cross and no bell on Bone 7, 8 or 9; Bone 10 (Day 309), the month's first Account-day and market, stood one morning beyond the close and was not brought into a day that had not earned it. Bone went on without a boundary drawn and neither of the district's two habits of naming its months was corrected. A hare went off toward \place{the north gate road} with its carrying answered by the weather, a question about the month's cost was answered with a direction, and the yard went around its seven walked-around places with a look and a wide step and no number (T077, T009/T013/T092). At \place{the yard well} on Bone 7 \person[Ilva]{Ilva} was seen once drawing with the pail against her knee, clay pale in her nail crescents and rack dust in one thumb seam, her hand going toward her sleeve and staying there with the notched strip unread, answering *Ask the pot what it cost. I keep the count, not the account*; on Bone 8 and 9 she was offstage (T080 held at nine). \person{Sella Vail} went to the fire before the basket at both ends of every day, told the coals it was the thaw and said *The house will keep it*, turned them with the back of a broken pot, lifted the twice-mended pot with her thumb where the wire crossed the crack and kept the crack showing, turned the dressed grain beneath the wool with a lighter touch and no figure, and set the pot rim two inches beyond the reaching hand with a half-beat and no look toward the bed; she touched \lore{the widow's mark} at no hour, because none of these was an opening (T058/T079, T077). The stayed frost gone off the well's north lip, the skinned trough and the silt-clouded plate on its flat stone stayed weather entered nowhere with no fourth likeness made a cause (T083/T085 held). **The chapter's one change was made in a place already charted:** at the going of the light on Bone 8, while Jorra's bent finger stood in the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, \person{Tarn}'s broom checked half a beat behind it on \place{the flagstones} and went on toward the road without a word or a turn of the head — the Seed 22 half-check and the Day 171 flagstone movement returned changed after a hundred and thirty-six days.
